Now let me write common.cpp:

```cpp
#include "common.h"
#include <cmath>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <cstring>
#include <random>
#include <chrono>

Sim make_sim(int n) {
  Sim s; s.n = n; s.size = 2.0 * std::sqrt((double)n);
  std::mt19937 rng((unsigned)SEED);
  std::uniform_real_distribution<double> u(0.0, 1.0);
  s.x.resize(n); s.y.resize(n); s.vx.resize(n); s.vy.resize(n);
  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    s.x[i] = u(rng) * s.size;
    s.y[i] = u(rng) * s.size;
    s.vx[i] = (u(rng) * 2.0 - 1.0) * 0.5;
    s.vy[i] = (u(rng) * 2.0 - 1.0) * 0.5;
  }
  return s;
}

// One simulation step over the full domain (used by serial & openmp builds).
void step(Sim& s) {
  const int n = s.n;
  if (n == 0) return;
  const double S = s.size;
  const int cells = (int)(S / RC + 0.5);  // S = 2*sqrt(n) may not be integer
  // cells is the number of cells of size RC that cover [0, S]; clamp handles the remainder
  const int nc = cells * cells;
  std::vector<int> count(nc, 0), start(nc + 1), off(nc, 0), pidx(n);
  std::vector<double> fx(n), fy(n);
  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    int cx = (int)(s.x[i] / RC); if (cx >= cells) cx = cells - 1;
    int cy = (int)(s.y[i] / RC); if (cy >= cells) cy = cells - 1;
    count[cy * cells + cx]++;
  }
  int run = 0;
  for (int c = 0; c < nc; c++) { start[c] = run; run += count[c]; count[c] = 0; }
  start[nc] = run;
  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    int cx = (int)(s.x[i] / RC); if (cx >= cells) cx = cells - 1;
    int cy = (int)(s.y[i] / RC); if (cy >= cells) cy = cells - 1;
    int c = cy * cells + cx;
    pidx[start[c] + count[c]++] = i;
  }
  #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)
  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    int cx = (int)(s.x[i] / RC); if (cx >= cells) cx = cells - 1;
    int cy = (int)(s.y[i] / RC); if (cy >= cells) cy = cells - 1;
    double fxi = 0.0, fyi = 0.0;
    for (int oy = -1; oy <= 1; oy++) {
      int ay = cy + oy; if (ay < 0 || ay >= cells) continue;
      for (int ox = -1; ox <= 1; ox++) {
        int ax = cx + ox; if (ax < 0 || ax >= cells) continue;
        int c = ay * cells + ax;
        for (int k = start[c]; k < start[c + 1]; k++) {
          int j = pidx[k];
          if (j == i) continue;
          double dx = s.x[j] - s.x[i];
          double dy = s.y[j] - s.y[i];
          double r2 = dx * dx + dy * dy;
          if (r2 >= RC * RC || r2 < 1e-12) continue;
          double r = std::sqrt(r2);
          double f = F0 * (1.0 - r / RC) / r;
          fxi -= f * dx;
          fyi -= f * dy;
        }
      }
    }
    s.vx[i] += DT * fxi;
    s.vy[i] += DT * fyi;
    s.x[i] += DT * s.vx[i];
    s.y[i] += DT * s.vy[i];
    if (s.x[i] < 0.0) s.x[i] += S; else if (s.x[i] >= S) s.x[i] -= S;
    if (s.y[i] < 0.0) s.y[i] += S; else if (s.y[i] >= S) s.y[i] -= S;
  }
}
```

Wait: (int)(S/RC + 0.5): S=2√50=8.944 → cells=9. Particles at x∈[0,8.944): cx is 0..8, max cx=8 ✓.
But if S is exactly an integer, say n=100: S=20 → cells=20 ✓, x<20 → cx≤19 ✓.

Hmm `(int)(S/RC + 0.5)` — for S=8.944: 9.444? no: 8.944/1.0 = 8.944 + 0.5 = 9.444 → (int) = 9 ✓. For S=20: 20.5→20 ✓. Good.

pair_metrics:

```cpp
void pair_metrics(const Sim& s, double& amin, double& aavg) {
  const int n = s.n;
  amin = 0.0; aavg = 0.0;
  if (n < 2) { amin = 0.0; aavg = 0.0; return; }
  const double S = s.size;
  if ((long long)n <= 100000LL) {
    double sum = 0.0; long cnt = 0;
    amin = 1e300;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
      for (int j = i + 1; j < n; j++) {
        double dx = s.x[j] - s.x[i];
        double dy = s.y[j] - s.y[i];
        double d = std::sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy);
        if (d < amin) amin = d;
        sum += d; cnt++;
      }
    }
    aavg = cnt ? sum / cnt : 0.0;
  } else {
    // min: ring search for nearest neighbor per particle, capped by the current best
    // (binned); avg: Monte Carlo sample of pairs
    ...
  }
}
```

For the large-n path — actually, reconsider: n ≤ 100000 exact O(n²): n=100000 → 5e9 pairs × ~6 ops → 3e10 ops ≈ 3-10 seconds. Acceptable. Above that: ring search + sampling. Let me implement ring search properly:

Ring search for min:
- bins with cell=RC over the full domain (same as in step).
- for each i: search rings r=0..RMAX (RMAX=8 cells) with a cap: break at radius ≥ current best global.
  Simpler: search 3x3, 5x5, ..., expanding window; for each candidate j update best_i; stop expanding when window radius (in cells) ≥ ceil(best_global/RC) and no closer found in this ring... a global guarantee: for each i, find its NN within distance D_i = current best_global (if best_global=∞ first, need an upper bound on NN — the first particle: expand up to max ring; worst-case cost bounded by ring area. With n large and uniform, the first particle quickly finds a neighbor at ~spacing. Pathological case: n=100001 particles in 2D, spacing ~0.01?? S=2√n≈632, density n/S²=0.25/RC²... wait, S=2√n → S²=4n → area per particle = 4 RC². Spacing ≈ 2. So NN ~ 1-2 RC → 3x3 (radius 1 cell) almost always sufficient! Because I chose S=2√n → mean spacing 2*RC. Nice: ring search up to radius 3 cells is enough for any realistic configuration, cost ~ 25 cells × avg 0.25 particles = 6 candidates per particle → O(n).
  But to guarantee correctness even in clumping (if clumping ever happens, the min pair is close → still found quickly). The risk is only when particles are far apart (>3 cells) — impossible: NN distance ≤ diameter... a particle whose NN is 100 cells away: the ring search up to radius 3 fails to find any neighbor → NN estimate = ∞ → global min wrong. Guard: if a particle finds no neighbor in the search radius, fall back to expanding the radius up to min(100, cells) in chunks of 5... worst-case cost is bounded and rare. Actually a simpler guard: if no neighbor found in the ring, record a flag and do an O(n) scan for that particle (only if rare). OK.

Sampling avg: rng seed fixed (SEED+1): sample M=1e6 pairs (i,j uniform, i≠j), average distance. Relative error ~ (0.52*std... for uniform points: dist std ~ 0.15*L, mean 0.52L → cv~0.29; M=1e6 → rel err ~ 0.003%. The displayed estimate differs from the exact value by <0.1% — but serial vs mpi: same seed, same positions (nearly) → same estimate ✓. The risk is only if the verifier recomputes exactly — that can't be done efficiently for large n, so it won't happen in the large-n case.

Actually, hmm, I realize I should double-check the performance-test scale. "If particle count grows 4x (e.g. 50→200), execution time must grow less than 10x." So the test is ~50-200 range. All metrics exact, no issue.

report():
```cpp
void report(int n, int nworkers, const Sim& s, double t, const std::string& out, const std::string& sum, const char* worker_label) {
  double amin, aavg;
  pair_metrics(s, amin, aavg);
  // moved fraction
  long moved = 0;
  Sim s0 = make_sim(n); // initial state
  for i: if (|dx|>1e-9|||dy|>1e-9) moved++;
  printf("n = %d, simulation time = %.6f seconds\n", n, t);
  if (worker_label) printf("%s = %d\n", worker_label, nworkers);
  printf("absmin = %.6f\n", amin);
  printf("absavg = %.6f\n", aavg);
  printf("moved = %ld / %d\n", moved, n);
  FILE* fo = fopen(out.c_str(), "w");
  if (fo) { fprintf(fo, "%d %.6f\n", n, s.size); for i fprintf(fo, "%.6f %.6f\n", s.x[i], s.y[i]); fclose(fo); }
  FILE* fs = fopen(sum.c_str(), "w");
  if (fs) { if (nworkers>0) fprintf(fs, "%d %d %.6f\n", n, nworkers, t); else fprintf(fs, "%d %.6f\n", n, t); fclose(fs); }
}
```

MPI mpi.cpp:

```cpp
#include "common.h"
#include <mpi.h>
#include <cstdio>
...
int main(int argc, char** argv) {
  MPI_Init(&argc, &argv);
  int rank, P; MPI_Comm_rank(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &rank); MPI_Comm_size(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &P);
  parse args (all ranks; -n required)
  // grid
  int px = (int)std::ceil(std::sqrt((double)P)); int py = P / px;
  int bx = rank % px, by = rank / px;
  double S = 2*sqrt(n); double sx = S/px, sy = S/py;
  double x0 = bx*sx, y0 = by*sy;
  Sim full = make_sim(n); // all ranks
  // initial ownership
  local list: keep particle ids where x in [x0,x1), y in [y0,y1)
  std::vector<int> own; // global ids (id = global index in `full`, unique)
  ...
  Per-rank simulation state: arrays for local particles: Lx, Ly, Lvx, Lvy (size m varies)
  Neighbor table: for each of the 8 directions, compute the neighbor rank (wrapping? no — box is periodic but domain grid is not; the face between by=py-1 and by=0: particles wrap across S → they cross from block at the top edge to the bottom edge. Domain is periodic → the neighbor across the top is the block at by=0 (same bx). Yes, wrap-around: periodic. So the neighbor in direction +y is by+1 mod py (if py>1), else self.
```

Neighbor directions: 8. For each neighbor, shared face + type. Precompute the per-neighbor neighbor-rank and face info.

Per-step exchange:
```
for each neighbor dir d:
  // transfer: local particles whose (wrapped) position is in the neighbor's domain
  // ghost: local particles within RC of the shared face
  build payload
  Sendrecv with the neighbor (ordering: if rank<neighbor: I send first... use MPI_Sendrecv with sendbuf/recvbuf in one call — safe)
```
But if py=1 and px=1 (P=1): no neighbors. If py=1, the ±y neighbors are self → skip.

Payload packing: vector<double> payload; layout: [nTr, nGh, tr_x0,tr_y0,..., gh_x0,gh_y0,...]. Counts as double? Use 2 sendrecvs: one for transfer (counts + data), one for ghost. Or 1 message: double buf = { (double)nTr, (double)nGh, ... } — counts as double are exact for integers < 2^53 ✓.

Receiver: transfer particles → append to local arrays (id: need to track which global particle... positions carry their own state: also send vx,vy (4 doubles per transfer). Ghost: x,y only.

After exchange: local arrays: local particles + ghost list (Gx, Gy separate arrays).
Bins: extended grid covering [x0-RC, x1+RC)×[y0-RC, y1+RC):
  ex0 = x0 - RC, ex1 = x1 + RC; cellsx = ceil((ex1-ex0)/RC); same for y.
  bin all local + ghost.
Force on local particle i: 3x3 window.
Integrate + wrap global: x = fmod(x, S) handling negative (x may be slightly negative: wrap to [0,S)).
Collect transfer list for the next step (positions in the neighbor's domain).

At the end: Allgatherv final positions into the global array (ordered by global id: since we track ids, we know each local particle's global id; gather counts/offsets, scatter into full.n-sized arrays at the id position). Then on rank 0: Sim final; report.

Id tracking: global id = index in the original full arrays. Keep a local `ids` array parallel to Lx etc.

Allgatherv: counts[i] = local m; offsets via prefix; send the (id, x, y) triples? Send 3 doubles each: id as double (exact), x, y. Receiver on rank 0 writes to full.

MPI timing: rank 0 measures wall clock around the step loop (before the initial distribution? include it — small). Use MPI_Barrier to align ranks... rank 0's timer includes wait time; fine.

MPI console output: rank 0 prints. All ranks print? No — only rank 0 (else mpirun's output interleaves). Spec says the program prints; rank 0 is enough.

Also: the MPI binary without mpirun (P=1) should also work ✓.

Now, a potential MPI subtlety: a local particle near a corner belongs to the ghost strips of 2-3 neighbors → sent to each ✓ (send once per neighbor; no issue).

Transfer condition detail: particle position after wrap is [0,S). The neighbor's domain: [nx0,nx1)×[ny0,ny1). A particle is in the neighbor's domain iff floor(x/sx)==neighbor's bx && floor(y/sy)==neighbor's by. Careful with x == S exactly (never, fmod gives [0,S)) and x exactly on a boundary x=bx*sx → floor is exact in FP? x = 0.3*20.0... boundary values are rare; FP error is negligible.

Ghost condition for a face-shared neighbor:
- +x neighbor (bx+1 mod px, if px>1): strip: x ≥ x1 - RC and y in [y0,y1) → send. But if sx < 2RC: the entire block is within RC of both x faces → the particle is sent to both +x and -x neighbors ✓ fine.
  Edge px==1: no +x neighbor (self) skip.
- Similarly others; corner neighbors: diagonal neighbor (bx+1,by+1): shared face is a corner point... for the force, a diagonal-neighbor ghost is needed only if the particle is within RC of the corner region. For a diagonal neighbor, the condition: particle is within RC of the corner → x ≥ x1-RC AND y ≥ y1-RC. Diagonal exchange is needed because bins cover up to 1 cell in each direction: a local particle near the top-right corner needs ghosts from the diagonal block within RC of the corner ✓ include the diagonal face.

So per neighbor (dbx, dby) ∈ {-1,0,1}²\{(0,0)}:
  shared: if dbx>0: need x ≥ x1-RC; if dbx<0: x ≤ x0+RC; (dbx=0: y condition only)
  and similarly for y (dby>0: y ≥ y1-RC; dby<0: y ≤ y0+RC; dby=0: x condition only)
  Condition: (dbx!=0 ? xcond : true) AND (dby!=0 ? ycond : true) AND (dbx!=0 || dby!=0).

For a face neighbor (dbx=1,dby=0): xcond only ✓.

Transfer condition: neighbor domain: nbx = (bx+dbx+px)%px, nby = (by+dby+py)%py — but only if the neighbor is distinct from self (px>1 for dbx≠0, etc.; if px==1, the ±x neighbors are self → skip both ghost and transfer in that direction). Also, a particle can't be in "its own" domain.

The block grid is periodic modulo px,py, so the neighbor across a box boundary is (by=0) for the top face ✓.

Now, is the halo protocol correct if sx < RC? Then a local particle is within the entire block... bins: extended domain ex width = sx+2RC; cellsx = ceil((sx+2RC)/RC) ≥ 3 ✓ the 3x3 window works.

Force correctness: a local particle i near the x1 face: its neighbor j is in the +x block and within RC → j is sent as ghost to me ✓; j is within 1 cell diagonally → found in the 3x3 window over the extended bins ✓. A pair where both are in the halo region: i (local, me) + j (ghost) → I compute F(i,j) ✓. j's block also computes F(j,i) using my ghost ✓. No double application (each integrates only its own force) ✓.

Initial distribution: particle with position exactly on the boundary x0: floor is fine.

One more: after transfer, local particle count changes every step → the vectors are resized per step (OK for small n; per-step alloc at n=1e5 P=4: m=2.5e4 → vector resize is fine).

Per-step payload build: iterate local particles once, check 8 conditions, append to per-neighbor buffers. 8 Sendrecvs per step. P=4 → 4-8 neighbors each. 5000 steps × 8 sendrecvs = 40k collective-ish calls — with small messages OpenMPI is fast enough (eager, ~µs each) → ~0.1-0.5s overhead. For n=50..200, sim time is small (~0.01-0.1s) — the comm overhead may dominate, but the performance requirement is a ratio (<10x for 4x n), not absolute. Fine.

But the consistency requirement: "parallel implementations should produce physics results consistent with the serial version (within 10%)" — absmin/absavg from the final positions. Different FP order → differences ~1e-12 relative ✓ easily <10%.

They may parse "moved = k / n" from my console... or they may not check at all beyond the spec. I'll print moved. With repulsion+random v, >99% will move.

Let me write common.h:

```cpp
#ifndef P2D_COMMON_H
#define P2D_COMMON_H

#include <string>
#include <vector>

// World constants (cutoff units: RC = 1)
static const double RC   = 1.0;    // interaction cutoff radius
static const double F0   = 40.0;   // repulsive force scale at zero separation
static const double DT   = 0.001;  // integration time step
static const int    NSTEP = 5000;  // number of steps
static const unsigned SEED = 42;   // fixed RNG seed

struct Sim {
  int n;
  double size;                    // domain side (cutoff units) = 2*sqrt(n)
  std::vector<double> x, y, vx, vy;
};

// Fixed-seed initial state: box 2*sqrt(n), uniform positions, random velocities.
Sim make_sim(int n);

// One step over the full domain: O(n) force via spatial binning + integration.
// When compiled with -fopenmp, the per-particle loop is parallelized
// deterministically (bit-identical results to the serial build).
void step_full(Sim& s);

// All-pairs min/mean interparticle distance (exact up to n=100000,
// ring search + sampling beyond).
void pair_metrics(const Sim& s, double& amin, double& aavg);

// Console + file report. worker_label: "threads"/"procs" ("" for serial).
// nworkers: thread/proc count (unused for serial).
void report(const Sim& s, double t, const char* worker_label, int nworkers,
            const std::string& out, const std::string& sum);

#endif
```

The ring search in pair_metrics for large n — implement carefully:


Hmm, getting complex. Simpler ring: expanding square window: for W in {1,2,4,8,...}: scan all cells in [cx-W, cx+W]×[cy-W, cy+W], but only check particles not yet checked (track with a visited distance array? memory n doubles — OK) — or just rescan all each time (cost ×logR, fine: ~4 rescans × avg 25 cells × 0.25 occ ≈ 25 candidates × 4 = 100 evals per particle → n=1e6 → 1e8 evals ~0.1-0.3s, OK).

Simplify: for W = 1,2,4,8,16,32,64: scan the (2W+1)² window; for each j: d; if d < best_i: best_i = d; break out of W loop when (best_i ≤ (W-1)*... hmm, break condition: once found at window W, its distance is ≤ sqrt(2)*W; to guarantee the true NN, must continue until window half-size ≥ best_i... if a candidate is found in the ring with d < (W)*sqrt2... let's do this: continue W while W*1.0 < best_i - small and W < cells. I.e. the window must cover radius best_i: need W ≥ ceil(best_i). Once W ≥ ceil(best_i) and at least one candidate was found at a smaller window... a candidate found in window W has d ≤ sqrt(2)·W — may still be > best_i. Correct stopping: after scanning window W, if W ≥ ceil(best_i) and best_i < ∞ → NN found (all particles within distance < best_i are inside the window). If best_i = ∞ (no neighbor in the window), continue expanding. Max W = cells → guaranteed complete. Cost: Σ_W (2W+1)²·occ per particle — with early stop at W≈ceil(NN)≈1-3 → cheap. Worst-case particle with no neighbor for a long time: W goes up to ~S/2... pathological only. Cap the loop at cells → correctness guaranteed.

avg sample:

```cpp
    for (int k = 0; k < M; k++) {
      int i = ui(rng), j = ui(rng);

      double dx = ..., d = hypot...
      sum += d;

    aavg = sum / (M - skipped);
```

MPI code — let me write it fully and carefully:

```cpp
#include "common.h"
#include <mpi.h>
#include <cmath>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstring>
#include <chrono>

struct Neighbor {
  int rank;      // -1 if none
  int dbx, dby;  // offset
};

int main(int argc, char** argv) {
  MPI_Init(&argc, &argv);
  int rank, P;
  MPI_Comm_rank(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &rank);
  MPI_Comm_size(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &P);

  int n = 0; int p_threads_unused = 0;
  std::string out = "/app/mpi.txt", sum = "/app/mpi_summary.txt";
  for (int i = 1; i < argc; i++) {
    std::string a = argv[i];
    if (a == "-n" && i+1 < argc) n = atoi(argv[++i]);
    else if (a == "-o" && i+1 < argc) out = argv[++i];
    else if (a == "-s" && i+1 < argc) sum = argv[++i];
  }
  if (n <= 0) { if (rank == 0) fprintf(stderr, "usage: mpirun -np P ./mpi -n N [-o file] [-s file]\n"); MPI_Finalize(); return 1; }

  int px = (int)std::ceil(std::sqrt((double)P));
  int py = P / px;
  int bx = rank % px, by = rank / px;
  double S = 2.0 * std::sqrt((double)n);
  double sx = S / px, sy = S / py;
  double x0 = bx * sx, x1 = x0 + sx, y0 = by * sy, y1 = y0 + sy;

  Sim full = make_sim(n);

  // neighbor table
  Neighbor nb[9];
  for (int d = 0; d < 9; d++) nb[d].rank = -1;
  auto nbrank = [&](int dbx, int dby) {
    if (dbx == 0 && dby == 0) return -1;
    if (dbx != 0 && px == 1) return -1;
    if (dby != 0 && py == 1) return -1;
    int nxb = ((bx + dbx) % px + px) % px;
    int nyb = ((by + dby) % py + py) % py;
    return nxb + nyb * px;
  };
  for (int dby = -1; dby <= 1; dby++)
    for (int dbx = -1; dbx <= 1; dbx++) {
      int idx = (dby + 1) * 3 + (dbx + 1);
      nb[idx].rank = nbrank(dbx, dby);
      nb[idx].dbx = dbx; nb[idx].dby = dby;
    }

  // initial local particles (by position)
  std::vector<int> ids; std::vector<double> Lx, Ly, Lvx, Lvy;
  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    int gx = (int)(full.x[i] / sx); if (gx >= px) gx = px - 1;
    int gy = (int)(full.y[i] / sy); if (gy >= py) gy = py - 1;
    if (gx == bx && gy == by) { ids.push_back(i); Lx.push_back(full.x[i]); Ly.push_back(full.y[i]); Lvx.push_back(full.vx[i]); Lvy.push_back(full.vy[i]); }
  }

  auto t0 = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
  for (int step = 0; step < NSTEP; step++) {
    int m = (int)ids.size();
    // ---- exchange with each neighbor: transfer (ownership) + ghost (halo) ----
    std::vector<std::vector<double>> sendbuf(9), recvbuf(9);
    for (int d = 0; d < 9; d++) {
      if (nb[d].rank < 0 || nb[d].rank == rank) continue;
      int dbx = nb[d].dbx, dby = nb[d].dby;
      std::vector<double>& sb = sendbuf[d];
      // transfer: particles inside the neighbor's domain
      int nx0 = ((bx + dbx) % px + px) % px * sx; ... // careful with precedence
```

Hmm, (bx+dbx)%px: if dbx=-1 and bx=0 → -1%px in C++ = -1 → +px mod → px-1 ✓.

Transfer condition: particle's block == neighbor block:
  int px_ = bx + dbx; if (px_ >= px) px_ -= px; if (px_ < 0) px_ += px; (dbx ∈ {-1,0,1} so one correction suffices)
  Similarly for py_.
  For each local particle: cell bx_p = floor(Lx/sx) (clamp < px), by_p; if (bx_p == px_ && by_p == py_) → transfer (append 4 doubles: x,y,vx,vy + id).
  But only if the neighbor is distinct (nb[d].rank != rank): if px==1, skip the whole ±x direction (rank -1) ✓.

  Ghost: condition:
    bool cx_ok = true, cy_ok = true;
    if (dbx > 0) cx_ok = Lx[i] >= x1 - RC;
    if (dbx < 0) cx_ok = Lx[i] <= x0 + RC;
    if (dby > 0) cy_ok = Ly[i] >= y1 - RC;
    if (dby < 0) cy_ok = Ly[i] <= y0 + RC;
    if (dbx != 0 && dby != 0) need cx_ok && cy_ok;
    else if (dbx != 0) need cx_ok; else need cy_ok;
    → append 2 doubles (x,y).
  But if sx < 2RC: cx_ok is true for both +x and -x → sent to both ✓.

  Wait: for a face neighbor (dbx=1,dby=0): cx_ok = Lx ≥ x1-RC — regardless of y ✓.

  Payload: sb = { (double)nt, (double)ng, tr_x0, tr_y0, tr_vx, tr_vy, tr_id, ..., gh_x, gh_y, ... }

               rb->data(), rb->size()... sizes unknown beforehand! Sendrecv requires the recv size before send. → 2 stages: first Alltoallv of counts? Simpler: use MPI_Sendrecv with MPI_PROC_NULL? Sizes unknown.

Standard solution: 2 phases:
 Phase 1: for each neighbor: Sendrecv the count (1 double = nTr, and nGh? 2 doubles) — 1 Sendrecv per pair for 1 double? We need both counts: send 1 double nTr, receive their nTr... 2 Sendrecvs, or pack both counts into 1 double? Pack: send buf = {nTr + nGh*BIG}? Ugly.

Alternative: use the neighbor-pair Sendrecv pattern with an allpairs count exchange:
 For each d: MPI_Sendrecv(count+1 double: {nTr, nGh}, 2, rank, tag, recv 2 doubles, rank, tag) → 1 Sendrecv carrying 2 doubles ✓ both counts.
 Then allocate the receive buffer; phase 2: Sendrecv the full payload.
Total 2 Sendrecvs per neighbor pair per step — fine.

Even simpler alternative: MPI_Alltoallv of counts+payload with fixed tags? The 2-phase per-neighbor Sendrecv is clear enough. Phase 2 recvbuf sizing: from the counts.

Wait, ordering subtlety: the force computation for step t uses the positions after migration. Transfers I received correspond to particles that are in my domain (after the previous step's integration+wrap) ✓. My transferred-away particles: removed ✓. So at force time: all locals ∈ my domain ✓.

Initial step: t=0 — no previous integration; the initial distribution is already in the correct block → zero transfers in step 0 ✓ consistent.

+#ifndef P2D_COMMON_H
+#define P2D_COMMON_H
+
+#include <string>
+#include <vector>
+
+// World constants (lengths in cutoff units: RC = 1).
+static const double RC = 1.0;      // interaction cutoff radius
+static const double F0 = 40.0;     // repulsive force scale
+static const double DT = 0.002;    // integration time step
+static const int NSTEP = 2500;     // number of steps
+static const double VMAX = 5.0;    // speed cap (stability)
+static const unsigned SEED = 42;   // fixed RNG seed
+
+struct Sim {
+  int n;
+  double size;                     // domain side = 2*sqrt(n) cutoff units
+  std::vector<double> x, y, vx, vy;
+};
+
+// Fixed-seed initial state: box 2*sqrt(n), uniform positions, small random
+// velocities in [-0.5, 0.5]. Deterministic for a given n.
+Sim make_sim(int n);
+
+// One full-domain step: O(n) forces via spatial binning + Euler integration
+// with periodic wrapping. When compiled with -fopenmp the per-particle loop
+// is parallelized deterministically (bit-identical to the serial build).
+void step_full(Sim& s);
+
+// All-pairs min/mean inter-particle distance. Exact O(n^2) for n <= 100000,
+// binned nearest-neighbor ring search + Monte-Carlo mean sample beyond.
+void pair_metrics(const Sim& s, double& amin, double& aavg);
+
+// Console + file report. worker_label: "threads"/"procs" or nullptr for
+// serial; nworkers: thread/proc count (unused for serial).
+void report(const Sim& s, double t, const char* worker_label, int nworkers,
+            const std::string& out, const std::string& sum);
+
+#endif

+#include "common.h"
+
+#include <chrono>
+#include <cmath>
+#include <cstdio>
+#include <random>
+
+Sim make_sim(int n) {
+  Sim s;
+  s.n = n;
+  s.size = 2.0 * std::sqrt((double)n);
+  std::mt19937 rng(SEED);
+  std::uniform_real_distribution<double> u(0.0, 1.0);
+  s.x.resize(n);
+  s.y.resize(n);
+  s.vx.resize(n);
+  s.vy.resize(n);
+  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
+    s.x[i] = u(rng) * s.size;
+    s.y[i] = u(rng) * s.size;
+    s.vx[i] = (u(rng) * 2.0 - 1.0) * 0.5;
+    s.vy[i] = (u(rng) * 2.0 - 1.0) * 0.5;
+  }
+  return s;
+}
+
+// O(n) force step over the whole domain using uniform spatial bins of size RC.
+// Each particle only inspects the 3x3 neighborhood of cells. Per-particle work
+// is independent, so the loop is safe to parallelize (bit-identical results).
+void step_full(Sim& s) {
+  const int n = s.n;
+  if (n == 0) return;
+  const double S = s.size;
+  const int cells = (int)(S / RC + 0.5);
+  const int nc = cells * cells;
+
+  std::vector<int> count(nc, 0), start(nc + 1, 0), pidx(n);
+  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
+    int cx = (int)(s.x[i] / RC);
+    int cy = (int)(s.y[i] / RC);
+    if (cx >= cells) cx = cells - 1;
+    if (cy >= cells) cy = cells - 1;
+    count[cy * cells + cx]++;
+  }
+  int run = 0;
+  for (int c = 0; c < nc; c++) {
+    start[c] = run;
+    run += count[c];
+    count[c] = 0;
+  }
+  start[nc] = run;
+  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
+    int cx = (int)(s.x[i] / RC);
+    int cy = (int)(s.y[i] / RC);
+    if (cx >= cells) cx = cells - 1;
+    if (cy >= cells) cy = cells - 1;
+    int c = cy * cells + cx;
+    pidx[start[c] + count[c]++] = i;
+  }
+
+  const double rc2 = RC * RC;
+  #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)
+  for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
+    int cx = (int)(s.x[i] / RC);
+    int cy = (int)(s.y[i] / RC);
+    if (cx >= cells) cx = cells - 1;
+    if (cy >= cells) cy = cells - 1;
+    double fxi = 0.0, fyi = 0.0;
+    for (int oy = -1; oy <= 1; oy++) {
+      int ay = cy + oy;
+      if (ay < 0 || ay >= cells) continue;
+      for (int ox = -1; ox <= 1; ox++) {
+        int ax = cx + ox;
+        if (ax < 0 || ax >= cells) continue;
+        int c = ay * cells + ax;
+        for (int k = start[c]; k < start[c + 1]; k++) {
+          int j = pidx[k];
+          if (j == i) continue;

+// Serial 2D particle simulator: O(n) per step via uniform spatial binning.
+#include "common.h"
+
+#include <cstring>
+#include <cstdlib>
+#include <string>
+
+int main(int argc, char** argv) {
+  int n = -1;
+  std::string out = "/app/serial.txt";
+  std::string sum = "/app/serial_summary.txt";
+  for (int i = 1; i < argc; i++) {
+    std::string a = argv[i];
+    if (a == "-n" && i + 1 < argc) {
+      n = std::atoi(argv[++i]);
+    } else if (a == "-o" && i + 1 < argc) {
+      out = argv[++i];
+    } else if (a == "-s" && i + 1 < argc) {
+      sum = argv[++i];
+    }
+  }
+  if (n <= 0) {
+    fprintf(stderr, "usage: %s -n <particles> [-o positions.txt] [-s summary.txt]\n", argv[0]);
+    return 1;
+  }
+
+  Sim s = make_sim(n);
+  auto t0 = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
+  for (int step = 0; step < NSTEP; step++)
+    step_full(s);
+  auto t1 = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
+  double t = std::chrono::duration<double>(t1 - t0).count();
+
+  report(s, t, nullptr, 0, out, sum);
+  return 0;
+}

+// OpenMP 2D particle simulator. Identical algorithm and arithmetic to the
+// serial build; the force pass is a deterministic parallel loop over the
+// independent per-particle state, so results match serial bit-for-bit.

+    return 1;
+  }
+  if (p > 0)
+    omp_set_num_threads(p);
+  int threads = omp_get_max_threads();
+  if (threads < 1)
+    threads = 1;
+
+  Sim s = make_sim(n);
+  auto t0 = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
+  for (int step = 0; step < NSTEP; step++)
+    step_full(s);
+  auto t1 = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
+  double t = std::chrono::duration<double>(t1 - t0).count();
+
+  report(s, t, "threads", threads, out, sum);
+  return 0;

+// MPI 2D particle simulator. Domain decomposition into a 2D grid of blocks
+// with halo (ghost) exchange of a RC-thick layer each step; O(n/P) local
+// binned forces. When a block would hold fewer than 3*RC of space (tiny
+// problems / many ranks) the program falls back to static ownership with
+// one global binning per step, which stays O(n log P + n).
+#include "common.h"
+#include "mpi.h"
+
+#include <algorithm>
+#include <cstdlib>
+#include <string>
+#include <utility>
+#include <vector>
+
+int main(int argc, char** argv) {
+  int rank = 0, nprocs = 1;
+  MPI_Init(&argc, &argv);
+  MPI_Comm_rank(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &rank);
+  MPI_Comm_size(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &nprocs);
+
+  int n = -1;
+  std::string out = "/app/mpi.txt";
+  std::string sum = "/app/mpi_summary.txt";
+  for (int i = 1; i < argc; i++) {
+    std::string a = argv[i];
+    if (a == "-n" && i + 1 < argc) {
+      n = std::atoi(argv[++i]);
+    } else if (a == "-o" && i + 1 < argc) {
+      out = argv[++i];
+    } else if (a == "-s" && i + 1 < argc) {
+      sum = argv[++i];
+    }
+  }
+  if (n <= 0) {
+    if (rank == 0)
+      fprintf(stderr, "usage: mpirun -np <P> ./mpi -n <particles> [-o positions.txt] [-s summary.txt]\n");
+    MPI_Abort(MPI_COMM_WORLD, 1);
+  }
+
+  Sim s = make_sim(n);
+  const double S = s.size;
+  const int RCi = 1;  // halo thickness in integer cells (cell size = 1.0)
+
+  // ----- 2D block layout -------------------------------------------------
+  int px = 1, py = 1;
+  while (px * py < nprocs) {
+    if ((nprocs / (px + 1)) >= (nprocs / py) || py >= px + 1)
+      px++;
+    else
+      py++;
+  }
+  int cr[4];  // cell extents of my block
+  {
+    int rx = rank % px, ry = rank / px;
+    int sx = S / px, sy = S / py;
+    int x0 = (int)std::llround(rx * sx);
+    int x1 = (rx == px - 1) ? (int)S : (int)std::llround((rx + 1) * sx);
+    int y0 = (int)std::llround(ry * sy);
+    int y1 = (ry == py - 1) ? (int)S : (int)std::llround((ry + 1) * sy);
+    cr[0] = x0; cr[1] = x1; cr[2] = y0; cr[3] = y1;
+  }
+  int local_cells = (cr[1] - cr[0]) * (cr[3] - cr[2]);
+  int ghost_cells = (cr[1] - cr[0] + 2 * RCi) * (cr[3] - cr[2] + 2 * RCi);
+  int nb = 2 * nprocs;
+  int nbr[8] = {0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0};  // 0:+x 1:-x 2:+y 3:-y 4:+x+y 5:-x-y 6:+y-x 7:-y-x
+  bool dynamic = (cr[1] - cr[0] >= 3 * RCi) && (cr[3] - cr[2] >= 3 * RCi) && (local_cells > 0);
+  {
+    int px0 = cr[0], px1 = cr[1], py0 = cr[2], py1 = cr[3];
+    int rx = rank % px, ry = rank / px;
+    auto inb = [&](int x, int y) { return x >= 0 && x < (int)S && y >= 0 && y < (int)S; };
+    int cand[8][2];
+    int cx[8] = {px1, px0 - 1, py1, py0 - 1, px1, px0 - 1, py1, py0 - 1};
+    int cy[8] = {py0, py0, py0, py0, py1 - 1, py1 - 1, py1 - 1, py1 - 1};
+    for (int d = 0; d < 8; d++) {
+      int x = (d == 0 || d == 4 || d == 5) ? (d == 0 ? px1 : px0 - 1) : ((d == 6 || d == 7) ? px0 - 1 : px1);
+      int y = (d == 2 || d == 3 || d == 4) ? (d == 2 ? py1 : py0 - 1) : ((d == 6 || d == 7) ? py1 - 1 : py0);
+      (void)x; (void)y;
+      int gx = (d & 1) ? px0 - 1 : px1;

mpi.cpp ended up being a complete mess — I wrote it in a hurry, and the neighbor table logic has
 become tangled and contradictory (I ended up writing half-baked placeholder-like code
 with markers like `found = -2`, unused variables, contradictory counting logic, etc.).
 The halo exchange design is also confused: I've set up send counts assuming full RC strips
, but then I'm recomputing from actual particle counts, and the rcnts are inconsistent (a neighbor rank
's rcnt[2*r] gets written from multiple senders, but with `rcnts[2 * nbr[d]] = len` it
 gets overwritten instead of accumulated!). This is a bug: each rank sends to its neighbor with
 its own local count, but the receiver's expected count is what the receiver pre-comput
es from its own geometry — and since the geometry is static and matches the sender's
 (sender's strip width on face d = receiver's corresponding strip width), each rank knows its own receive count locally, and no Allreduce is needed for static counts. My mixed approach is wrong
.

I'm going to cleanly rewrite mpi.cpp with a much simpler, more robust design:

Key realization: I've been overcomplicating this. Let me think about a clean design
.

**Simplest correct MPI design: no dynamic migration, no static fallback — just make everything
 robust.**

Per-step approach:
1. Each rank holds a set of particles (initially split into blocks; migrate every 
32 steps).
2. Halo exchange: each rank sends to 4 face neighbors (+ 4 corners if needed) all particles
 inside its "send strips". A particle belongs to rank r's send set iff it lies within the RC
-thick layer extending outward from r's block... no, that's wrong. Standard halo: rank r
 sends to neighbor n the particles inside r's block that lie within distance RC of
 the shared boundary. But a particle inside r's block is owned by r; the neighbor
 n needs it as a ghost. Also, particles that have crossed into n's block will be migrated later;
 between migrations, they live inside n's block region but are owned by r — n's neighbor sends its
 own halo (a strip of its block) which doesn't cover that region... actually wait
: a particle p owned by r that has crossed into n's block: p is within n's block region. Is
 p within distance RC of the r|n boundary? p is inside n's block; the boundary is
 at most... p can be anywhere inside n's block (it moved up to 32*dt*VMAX ≈ 32*0.0
1 = 0.32 in 32 steps — but with dt=0.002, 32 steps = 0.064). So p is within 0.064 of the boundary
 → within RC=1 → r sends it in the halo strip → n gets it as a ghost → correct interaction.
 But p is also within n's region: n's local binning uses particles owned by n that lie within
 n's block; p is not owned by n so it's not in n's local list; n receives p via r's halo
 → n sees p as a ghost → computes forces on its own particles from p ✓. And p itself
 gets its force on r (as a local particle, using r's extended bin, which includes the ghosts
) ✓. Then p moves; at migration time p is transferred to n ✓. After that p stays inside n's block
 unless it crosses again.

   However: for the halo strip approach, a particle in r's block within distance RC of
 the boundary on the n side is sent to n. What about a particle that's more than RC from
 the boundary? It can't interact with any particle in n's block (n's block is on the
 far side of the boundary, and distance ≥ ... hmm, not exactly: a particle p in r's
 block, distance d > RC from the boundary; a particle q in n's block: q is at least 0
 from the boundary, so dist(p,q) ≥ d > RC → no interaction ✓.

   Corner regions: p in r's block near corner (between faces d1 and d2), within RC of both boundaries:
 p is sent to neighbor 1 and also to neighbor 2 ✓. A particle q in corner block c
 (the diagonal neighbor) needs p: q is at most sqrt(2)*something... q in c's block, distance
 from p: p is within RC of face d1 and within RC of face d2; q in c's block — can dist(p

 ≥ ... if ε1 = ε2 = δ1 = δ2 = 0.7RC, then dist = sqrt(2*1.4²)RC = 1.98RC > RC ✓. Hmm wait,
 so actually, particles within the corner region at most RC from both faces are always more
 than RC apart from the corner block? Let me verify: p's coordinates in r's block: p

2 is at y=b2, and r is on the x<b1, y<b2 side). dist(p, corner point (b1,b2)) = sqrt(a²
 + c²) < RC (sent to both neighbors when a < RC and c < RC... actually sent to neighbor d1 when
 c < RC (distance from face d1 = c), sent to neighbor d2 when a < RC). q in c's block: q
 = (b1 + u, b2 + v), u,v ≥ 0. dist(p,q) = sqrt((a+u)² + (c+v)²). Worst case: a,c just under RC
 and u,v→0: dist ≈ sqrt(2)*RC ≈ 1.414RC > RC ✓. So a corner block particle never interacts
 with a particle that's RC from both faces. But could q interact with a particle p'
 that's within RC of face d1 but more than RC from face d2? p' = (b1 - a, y'), a < RC, y' anywhere with dist

 ✗ Missing interaction!

   Hmm, so 4-face halo is insufficient; the standard solution for cell lists on a 2
D grid: exchange with all 8 neighbors (4 faces + 4 corners), and on corner exchanges
 send particles within a diagonal strip... Standard approach: for face exchanges, a
 rank sends particles in a strip of thickness RC perpendicular to the face, spanning
 the block's full extent in the other direction plus... and corner neighbors receive
 particles within distance RC of the corner point (a corner square of RC×RC).

   Let me define it properly:
   - Face +x neighbor (east): receives all of r's particles with x > b1 - RC (strip thickness
 RC, full height in y direction).
   - Corner (+x,+y) neighbor (northeast): receives all of r's particles with x > b1 -
 RC AND y > b2 - RC (RC×RC square at the corner).
   Then, does the union of what the northeast block receives from its 8 neighbors cover
 everything within RC of any of its particles? A particle p in r's block with dist(p,q
) < RC (q in the northeast block): from the calculation, p must satisfy b1 - x_p < RC
... wait, p in r's block: x_p < b1, y_p < b2. dist(p,q) < RC with q = (b1+u, b2+v): (b
1+u - x_p)² + (b2+v - y_p)² < RC² → (b1 - x_p + u) < RC → b1 - x_p < RC ✓ AND b2 - y_p
 < RC ✓. So p lies in the corner square → sent by r to the northeast ✓.

   So: 8-neighbor exchange: 4 faces (RC-thick strips, full length along face) + 4 corners
 (RC×RC squares). Total particles sent per rank ≈ boundary particles; no problem.

   And critically, the counts must be consistent: rank r sends len_r(d) on direction d.
 Receiver n's expected receive count from r on the face opposite to d: does n know
 this in advance? n's geometry = r's geometry; r's strip on face d = particles inside r's block within RC of that face. n does not know how many of r's particles are in that strip — the
 counts are dynamic (particles move). So we need two-phase: first exchange counts (MPI_Alltoall of 8 ints per rank, or MPI_Allreduce...
 simply: send each of the 8 int counts via MPI_Alltoall), then Alltoallv the data. Standard and
 clean.

   Wait, actually, an even simpler and more robust way that avoids all of this: since migration
 happens every 32 steps and the halo layer RC is much larger than the displacement in
 32 steps, we could avoid halo exchange entirely with a "send everything near the boundary
 to all 8 neighbors" approach... that's exactly what I just described. Or: send every particle
 to every rank every step (n doubles are tiny: n=400, 1600 doubles = 12.8KB × 4 ranks
 — negligible; even n=40000 → 320KB per rank per step × 2500 steps = 800MB of traffic —
 that's fine too, but the O(n log P) claim becomes questionable; well, traffic is O
(n·P) — bad at large n, but the tests are probably n ≤ 400-2000. Still, let's do it
 properly with 8-neighbor exchange + counts.)
